\caption{Paired RPL sensitivity exposes benefits and costs. Gap weight 0.5, 10\% capacity, all three fitting seeds. Lines show means of seed-relative inflation reductions and honest-error changes; failure changes are percentage points per listing. Bands are full seed ranges, not confidence intervals. Positive values are favorable only in panel (a). The table instead reports absolute means; its ratio-of-means percentages need not equal panel (a)'s mean of seed-relative percentages. No outcomes or weights are discarded.}
\label{fig:rpl_tradeoffs}
\end{figure}

\subsection{Five recent acquisition families on fixed price models}\label{sec:recent_results}
\noindent\textbf{Takeaway.} Estimator choice and simple strong controls remain
material after expanding recent-method coverage.

ACO, SEFA, GDFS, DIME and explanation-ranked AFA each covered three sources,
three fitting seeds, two frozen price backends and six capacities, totaling
540 common-grid cells; ACO additionally retained 36 fixed-neighbor sensitivities.
Within source/seed/backend/capacity, honest MAE agreed across these methods to
less than $10^{-6}$, as required when only acquisition changes. ACO's declared
$k=25$ was used throughout; no evaluation-best neighbor count replaced it.
Figure~\ref{fig:recent_curves} shows every family's budget curve alongside
available matched risk/benefit controls. At 10\% capacity with the robust
backend, GDFS had the lowest family-mean inflation on JUCars (0.01013),
whereas SEFA was lowest on MUCars (0.00660) and synthetic (0.01067). No recent family minimized inflation on all three sources.
Simple risk or benefit remained competitive or better in several mask-aware
comparisons. Full four-metric primary means are in
Appendix~\ref{app:completion_methods}; an inflation ranking is not a
multiobjective dominance result.

\caption{Five recent acquisition families under shared one-query batch capacity. Lines are means over three fitting seeds; shading is the full seed range, not a confidence interval. Rows fix the mask-aware or robust price predictor and columns denote sources. ACO uses fixed $k=25$. Risk and benefit use their available matching mask-aware backend; no matching robust-backend version is invented. All nonconverged outcomes remain included. The four classification-based adapters use training-only price bins, and every method has explicit task-specific adaptation boundaries.}
\label{fig:recent_curves}
\end{figure}

\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
\noindent\textbf{Takeaway.} The supported contribution is a bounded information
frontier, an audited recovery-path diagnosis and a targeted regularization
pilot. The original factorial identifies adverse effects of its implemented
feedback procedure; stronger-search interventions narrow that interpretation.

The detailed claim--evidence map is retained in Appendix~\ref{app:audit_map}.



The information frontier and recovery-path findings answer different questions.
The frontier limits what verification can guarantee under observationally
indistinguishable missingness; the recovery analysis shows how an otherwise
valid query can change the quotation adversely. Together they support auditing
both the information available to an allocator and the quotation after recovery.
The compact formulation supplies an exact finite reference, while the small
context-aware rule library shows why a solver advantage cannot be inferred
from a restricted comparator alone.

For deployment, the history cap is a conditional intervention whose attacked
error and failed-query effects need to be measured alongside inflation. The
source-specific SAV results similarly support local validation against static
robust and simple acquisition controls. They do not justify selecting SAV as
a universal default. The completed factorial experiment identifies initial-objective
effects on MUCars and adverse feedback effects across all three cohorts.
These controlled effects do not establish the cause of the original unmatched
SAV comparison. Section~\ref{sec:completion_mechanisms} directly varies response
search and shows that strengthening it removes part of the observed damage.
The RPL intervention further links a measured failure path to lower gain, while
its failed-query and honest-error costs keep the deployment claim conditional. Fitting-seed resampling alone cannot establish uncertainty over
new training populations.


The study does not infer actual seller behavior from the advertised-price
cohorts. Concealment sets, utility, query success and response search are
stipulated. Only two real cohorts, four query fields, unit costs and batches
of 100 are evaluated. New market conditions, noisy verification, multi-field
queries, nonunit costs and adaptive interrogation can change the frontier or
the observed ranking. These limits restrict deployment claims rather than
invalidate the explicitly audited finite comparisons.

\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
Budgeted verification has both an information limit and a quotation-path
consequence. Uniform honest precision restricts the concealment gain that can
be removed when natural and strategic absence generate the same report. A
history cap can suppress adverse recovery paths under fixed peers, while
recomputed responses expose attacked-error and failed-query costs. The matched
experiment further shows that a positive feedback--allocation interaction is
insufficient evidence of improvement: all 36 primary feedback comparisons
increase mean inflation at zero concealment cost in the original implementation,
even though robust initialization helps on MUCars. Stronger training-response
search removes some of that harm in matched development controls. RPL directly
penalizes gain-increasing recovery edges and reduces primary inflation in a
three-seed pilot, while predictive error, failed queries and nonconvergence
preclude a universal superiority claim. A three-seed positive-cost sensitivity reverses the synthetic top-$B$ feedback ranking alongside sharply reduced concealment and high query failure, further limiting generalization. The practical implication is to audit public information, recovery
semantics and every comparison component, retaining negative results and
multiobjective trade-offs. This conclusion is conditional on the implemented
learning and response procedures, not a universal prescription for valuation.

\section*{Data, code and use of AI tools}
The source datasets are publicly available under CC BY 4.0 through the
versioned Mendeley Data records ~\cite{jucarsdata,mucarsdata}. All reported
prices are advertisements. A private research repository contains the
implementation, full six-capacity analysis tables, resampling tensors, figure
source data and hash-indexed audit receipts. It is not yet a public archival
release. Model weights and large per-listing outputs remain on the research
machine. A fresh dependency environment on that same machine reproduced the 1,536 aggregate simultaneous-analysis rows within $10^{-12}$ tolerance and matched the threshold grids. This bounded replay does not establish external clean-machine reproduction or replicate model training.
The source access and reconstruction instructions are included in the evidence
package. External access and an archival release must be finalized before an
editorial submission requiring public availability.

AI assistance was used for implementation support, experiment orchestration,
analysis-code development, literature checking, figure preparation and manuscript
drafting. Quantitative claims are tied to saved experimental records and
the programmatic checks described above; AI-generated empirical values were not substituted for saved records. AI tools are not authors. Author names and affiliations are deliberately omitted from this version.

\appendix
\section{Contextual policy-library witnesses}\label{app:library}
\paragraph{A capacity-related global-mixture gap.}
Consider $n>B\ge1$, a fixed core $C$ of size $B$, and public contexts
$j\in\{B+1,\ldots,n\}$. In context $j$, potential quote gaps are 2 for core
items, 1 for item $j$, and $\epsilon=1/(B+2)$ for every other item. These are
linear toy utility units. Use the honest-exact missingness-alias worlds from
the preceding construction. All items are query-eligible in the all-missing report.

The reference library contains \emph{all} fixed item-priority orders and a risk
rule using these exact gaps, but employs one context-independent mixing vector.
Every priority rule selects a fixed $B$-subset. Risk always selects the core,
because $2>1>\epsilon$, without relying on a cutoff tie. Thus the global mixture
has the same query marginals in every context, whereas a publicly conditioned
mechanism may change them with $j$.

\begin{proposition}[Global-mixture separation]
In this family the optimal worst gains are
\[
 T_{\rm global}=\frac{2(n-B)}{2n-B},\qquad
 T_{\rm context}=\frac{2}{B+2}.
\]
Their ratio is $(B+2)(n-B)/(2n-B)$. Taking $n=B^2+B$ makes the ratio
$B(B+2)/(2B+1)$, which grows without bound with $B$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
A global mixture with value $t$ must satisfy $2(1-p_i)\le t$ for each core
item and $1-p_i\le t$ for each other item, using the context in which that
item is active. Summing gives $B\ge n-t(B/2+n-B)$ and the stated lower bound.
It is attained with core marginal $1-T_{\rm global}/2$ and other marginal
$1-T_{\rm global}$. These sum to $B$ and are implementable as a lottery over
$B$-subsets, all represented by fixed priorities. In one public context, the
$B$ core items and active item imply $B\ge B+1-t(B/2+1)$. Set core marginal
$(B+1)/(B+2)$, active-item marginal $B/(B+2)$ and all others zero to attain the
bound. The low-gap unqueried items have gain $\epsilon<T_{\rm context}$.
Both constructions use the preceding hard-budget sampling argument.
\end{proof}

We checked rational primal/lower-bound witnesses on 26 predetermined $(n,B)$
cases and 257,274 context--item rows. Sixteen small cases also match independently
constructed complete-subset LPs for both policy classes. At $B=20,n=420$, the
ratio is $440/41\approx10.73$. This is a constructed limitation of global mixing over the specified
context-insensitive rule library; it does not establish that real-source
CDM losses arise from this mechanism or that these bounds are first in the literature.

\paragraph{A stronger small-library comparator.}
The separation concerns the information used by the rules, not fixed mixture
weights alone. Add $B+1$ deterministic context-aware rules: one queries every
core item; rule $i$ replaces core item $i$ with the publicly active item.
Give the first rule weight $2/(B+2)$ and each replacement rule weight $1/(B+2)$.
The weights are fixed across contexts. Every draw uses exactly $B$ queries,
while each core item's marginal is $(B+1)/(B+2)$ and the active item's marginal
is $B/(B+2)$. Thus this small library attains $T_{\rm context}$ without solving
a new LP at deployment. The previous lower bound proves its optimality within
the constructed family. It does not imply an unseen-context guarantee for
learned rules on vehicle records.

An independent exact-arithmetic extension checks all 26 cases, 15,922
context--rule allocations and 257,274 context--item losses. Freezing the active
item or changing the weights to uniform fails the optimum check. Consequently,
this family supports a representation limitation of the original library,
not an intrinsic advantage of dynamically chosen weights or an LP solver.
This stronger comparator must be retained in subsequent algorithm evaluations.

\section{Conditional probability-stability diagnostics}\label{app:stability}
\noindent\textbf{Takeaway.} Randomized allocation admits a conditional
sensitivity bound, but small field margins and scores near zero limit its
applicability. Stability alone does not imply lower strategic inflation.

Fix the positive-score support $I$, the queried field of each item, and budget
$B$. For two score vectors $g,g'$ with all supported scores at least $m>0$, let
$\delta=\|g-g'\|_\infty$. Define the score-minimax value and canonical marginals
\[
 T(g)=\min_{0\leq p\leq1,\,\sum_i p_i\leq B}\max_{i\in I}g_i(1-p_i),
 \qquad p_i(g)=[1-T(g)/g_i]_+.
\]
For $B=0$ use zero marginals; for $B\geq |I|$ use unit marginals on $I$.
Then
\[
 |T(g)-T(g')|\leq\delta,\qquad
 \|p(g)-p(g')\|_\infty\leq\min\{1,2\delta/m\}.
\]
For any fixed feasible allocation, the two objectives differ by at most
$\delta$, which proves the value bound after minimization. The function
$h(t,x)=[1-t/x]_+$ is separately $1/m$-Lipschitz in $t$ and $x$ for
$t\geq0,x\geq m$: on its positive branch, $t/x^2\leq1/x$, and it is
continuous at the branch boundary. Applying the value bound proves the
marginal bound. The active allocation set may change; positive-score support
and queried fields must remain fixed.

If both pre-query and recovered quotes change by at most $\epsilon$, and the
primed recovery span is at most $R$, expected quotes differ by at most
$\epsilon+R\min\{1,2\delta/m\}$. If these conditions hold uniformly over
all own reports at fixed peer reports, costs are unchanged, and truthful
quotes are verification-neutral, the positive unilateral-gain envelope changes
by at most $2\epsilon+R\min\{1,2\delta/m\}$. This is not a guarantee for
adapted peer responses, equilibrium selection, training convergence, or a
fixed realization of the sampling randomness.

Three examples delimit the claim. With two items and $B=1$, swapping scores
$(1+\delta,1)$ and $(1,1+\delta)$ changes the first item's top-$B$ probability
by one, but its minimax probability by $\delta/(2+\delta)$. In contrast,
swapping $(2\delta,\delta)$ and $(\delta,2\delta)$ changes its minimax
probability by $1/3$ even as $\delta$ tends to zero. Finally, one fully queried
item with two fields whose scores swap as in the first example can have a
unit quote jump if the two recovery quotes are zero and one. The score lower
bound and fixed-field condition therefore cannot simply be removed.
An exact-arithmetic checker covers 2,916 score-pair/budget cases, including
510 active-set changes; 108 production/linear-program comparisons agree to
floating-point precision. These finite checks supplement the proof.

We additionally audited all 1,080 saved fixed-profile batch executions at
hypothetical radii $\delta=\alpha\operatorname{median}(g_i:g_i>0)$.
A sufficient certificate requires support preservation and every positive
item's top-two field gap to exceed $2\delta$. Its bound uses
$m=\min_{i\in I}g_i-\delta$. Recovery spans are reconstructed for every
eligible item, including items not queried by a saved top-$B$ allocation.
Table~\ref{tab:stability} records the sufficient-certificate counts.
\begin{table}[htbp]\centering
\begin{tabular}{rrrr}
\toprule
$\alpha$ & Minimax conditions & Probability bound $<1$ & Top-$B$ constant\\
\midrule
0.001 & 471 & 468 & 401\\
0.01 & 401 & 353 & 212\\
0.05 & 267 & 245 & 53\\
0.1 & 244 & 242 & 39\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\caption{Post-hoc stability certificates at four hypothetical relative score radii. Each row covers the same 1,080 dependent fixed-profile batch executions. Counts certify sufficient conditions, rather than observed perturbation errors or a ranking of performance.}\label{tab:stability}\end{table}
Each row has 1,080 batch executions, including both generating allocators;
these are dependent diagnostic cases, not independent statistical replicates.
Top-$B$ is locally constant when the same support/field conditions and its
selection-rank margin hold (or all positive items fit). Its certificate is
stronger than a nonzero sensitivity bound, so counts do not establish an
ordering of stability. Failure to certify is not evidence of instability.
A separate checker reconstructs all 4,320 condition rows and 144 grouped
summaries using exact fractions of the stored scores and radii; displayed
quote bounds remain floating-point approximations. The radii are not calibrated
prediction errors. These post-hoc diagnostics neither establish performance
improvement nor explain source effects causally. General water-filling
sensitivity is established literature; priority for this application-specific
bound is not claimed.

\section{Strong additional comparators and adaptation boundaries}\label{app:comparators}
The following controls belong to earlier separately corroborated experiments,
not the newly trained matched factorial model family. Keeping these identities
separate prevents a favorable acquisition or tuning result from being attributed
to the wrong training intervention.

\subsection{Ordinary-benefit and equal-allowance controls}
A robust/no-feedback price model with ordinary error-reduction acquisition was
evaluated on all six budgets and the original three seeds. The independently
checked 54-cell extension retained identical honest quotes, public scoring,
allocations and first-batch exact deviations. At 10\% capacity, reference
inflation was 0.01077, 0.01117 and 0.00778 on synthetic, JUCars and MUCars,
respectively. Reference-minus-SAV differences were $-0.00998$, $-0.00432$
and $0.00089$, with conditional shared-batch intervals
$[-0.01260,-0.00791]$, $[-0.00598,-0.00285]$ and $[0.00045,0.00130]$.
These intervals averaged the three fitted seeds before resampling batches;
they are not the ten-seed crossed intervals in the main factorial analysis.

An additional size/tuning study retained training caps 1,000, 2,500 and 5,000,
three fitting seeds, three training-internal gap-weight trials and twelve
residual epochs for both candidates. Its 972 parent tuning cells and 324
ordinary-benefit reference cells passed full audits. At the largest cap,
tuned SAV inflation was 20.41\% lower on MUCars but 30.09\% higher on
synthetic and 27.09\% higher on JUCars than the separately tuned ordinary-benefit
reference. The associated reference-minus-SAV intervals were
$[0.00097,0.00186]$, $[-0.00682,-0.00204]$ and $[-0.00333,-0.00125]$.
All scheduled caps and fixed/tuned roles remain in the archived evidence;
no favorable training size replaces the largest-cap comparison. This further
supports conditional gains rather than a consistent candidate advantage.

\subsection{Categorical support-margin imputation}
A whole-field adaptation of support-margin imputation (SMI)~\cite{smi} used
all allowed covariates and four optional-field groups. One deterministic
fitting generation per source trained a sixteen-model linear support-vector
regression family on complete-optional-field training rows. Scaling, category
vocabularies and target normalization were training-only. Missing categories
required a one-hot completion including an unknown token. If the reduced
sign-consistent model family or its optimization was unavailable, the method
used ordinary median/mode imputation. The original SMI guarantee is not
asserted on these fallback rows, and this is not an unchanged official reproduction.

An independent categorical-enumeration and scalar-interval auditor checked
94,527 report/domain cases and every final quotation and legal no-query report.
Training cohort sizes were 2,947, 2,674 and 3,368 for synthetic, JUCars and
MUCars. Across unrestricted, nonnegative and unit-box completion domains,
aggregate outcomes matched ordinary median/mode regression. Attacked fallback
fractions were 75.3\%, 87.3\% and 65.6\%, respectively. This diagnoses a limit
of the categorical adaptation's retained-family assumptions; it does not
refute the original SMI theorem. These no-query fits differ in cohort and
model class from the matched factorial predictor, so their higher inflation
cannot isolate a benefit of the present acquisition procedure.

\subsection{Acquisition-conditioned neighbor control}
ACO-1 adapted acquisition-conditioned expected-loss scoring~\cite{activeoracle}
to one-field regression queries and batch capacity. A training-only neighbor
bank estimated loss changes for sixteen observation patterns; unavailable
recorded bank values gave zero replay benefit. Distances used observed query
groups and permitted nonoptional features, with fixed category/numeric weights
and stable ties. It was a clean-room task adaptation, not an unchanged execution
of the original sequential classification method.

The separately corroborated nine-pair study contained 144 cells. The declared
main $k=25$ used every capacity; fixed $k=10$ and $k=50$ sensitivities used
10\% capacity only. No winning $k$ was selected. With the frozen robust
predictor and $k=25$, mean inflation was 0.01081 on synthetic, 0.01118 on
JUCars and 0.00716 on MUCars. Full-SAV-minus-ACO differences of 0.00995 and
0.00431 on synthetic and JUCars had conditional intervals
$[0.00725,0.01345]$ and $[0.00291,0.00588]$. On MUCars the point comparison
favored SAV by 0.00027, but the interval crossed zero. Thus a strong predictive
acquisition control can outperform the original candidate. These results
strengthen comparator coverage without implying that one-step ACO-1 dominates
the matched factorial policies or reproduces the original method's guarantee.

\section{Conditional uncertainty and mechanism diagnostics}\label{app:mechanism_diagnostics}
\subsection{Exact conditional crossed-bootstrap variance}
Let $Y$ be a paired-difference matrix with $S$ fitting seeds and $K$ batches.
Independent bootstrap seed weights $U$ and record-normalized batch weights
$V$ give $U^\top YV$. Write $m=\mathbb E[V]$, $C=\operatorname{Cov}(V)$,
$r=Ym-\mathbf1\,\operatorname{mean}(Ym)$,
$z=\operatorname{mean}_{s}(Y)$ and
$R=Y-\mathbf1z^\top-r\mathbf1^\top$. The conditional variance decomposes as
\[
 \frac{\|r\|^2}{S^2}+z^\top Cz+
 \frac{1}{S^2}\sum_s R_s^\top C R_s.
\]
These are seed-main, batch-main and interaction terms. Batch-weight moments
are calculated by conditioning on the multinomial count of the single
partial batch, retaining ratio normalization. Exhaustive balanced and
unequally weighted $2\times2$ resampling independently checks the formula.
All 48 capped/full real-source feedback decompositions are retained.
Capped components use ten equal 100-record batches; uncapped MUCars components
use 107 batches normalized by record counts, including a 72-record tail.
Thus the outcome matrix and averaging weights both change. For top-$B$ and
minimax, batch variances fall by factors 5.73 and 8.04 (not 10.7), while
seed-main variances change by $-13.20\%$ and $-9.43\%$. This is not variance
reduction from sample size alone. Holding all 107 batches fixed and changing
only equal-batch to record-count weights changes the seed term by merely
$-0.16\%$ and $+0.11\%$, respectively; the larger change cannot be attributed
to tail weighting alone.

Thirty-seed projections divide seed and interaction terms by three; the
batch-noise-free projection holds the empirical batch distribution fixed.
Neither supplies new training runs, equivalence evidence or population coverage.

\subsection{Concealment identification from saved utility summaries}
For one listing let $C_i=c_i k_i$ be concealment cost, $G_i$ positive expected
quote inflation, $N_i$ the positive best-legal-utility gain over truth, and
$\rho_i$ remaining unilateral regret. Since truthful utility is its honest
quote,
\[
 C_i-\rho_i\le G_i-N_i\le C_i.
\]
Thus $\max(\overline G-\overline N,0)$ bounds mean cost below; adding
$\max_i\rho_i$ and capping by $2\overline c$ bounds it above. Uniform-cost
hidden-field bounds divide by $c$; heterogeneous costs in $[.01,.03)$ give
bounds using .03 and .01, respectively. Dividing by mean heterogeneous cost
would not identify mean hidden fields. Positive-cost states were not saved;
all 20 nonconverged batches remain in the bounds. The inequality passes
768 exhaustive toy utility/returned-state checks. The paired zero-cost
concealment counts use saved states directly. Neither co-occurrence nor this
accounting identity identifies a causal mediation effect.

\subsection{Restricted final-record honest-error pilot}
Three frozen robust/no-feedback models per real source (seeds 13, 42 and
2026) enumerate completions of transmission, fuel and body condition from
their training-encoder vocabularies, including missing tokens. Mileage must
be observed and fixed; other covariates stay fixed. Unsupported observed
categories and missing mileage abstain. The same original 1,000 records per
source are used without tuning or retraining. Current records are included
in the minima; singleton sets preserve their original quotes exactly.
Shared-support errors and abstentions are reported separately.

\caption{The restricted final-record envelope's honest-error cost is concentrated on naturally incomplete categorical records. (a) Among each source's original 1,000 listings, complete/incomplete/abstained counts are 800/1/199 for JUCars and 971/28/1 for MUCars. Complete means all three declared categorical fields are observed; missing mileage causes abstention. Complete covered records have singleton completion sets and exactly zero change. (b) Every MUCars fitting seed is shown for all 999 covered records and its nested 28-record naturally incomplete subset; lines link the two descriptive means for the same frozen model, without interpolation or confidence intervals. Positive honest-MAE change is worse; units are MUCars currency. Mileage remains observed and fixed. This does not validate the full four-field game or unseen-category coverage.}
\label{fig:envelope_boundary}
\end{figure}

Each JUCars model evaluates 806 completions; each MUCars model evaluates
1,139. All baseline quotes match saved predictions within $5\times10^{-6}$
in log quote; sampled completions also match the production all-state
inference route within that tolerance. Predictor primitives are shared.
On the declared covered domain, the envelope is exactly costless for complete
categorical records: 800/801 JUCars and 971/999 MUCars records have singleton
completion sets. All quotation and error changes are confined to the one and
28 naturally incomplete records, respectively. For the MUCars subset, the
largest individual quotation reduction is 25.5\% of that record's original
predicted quote, while mean honest-MAE increases range from 374.24 to 851.51
currency units (Table~\ref{tab:final_record_pilot}). Quotation discounts and
absolute-error costs have different denominators and must not be conflated.
The practical trade-off is zero change on complete covered categorical
records but potentially substantial discounts on naturally incomplete ones.
The guarantee covers only
the declared categorical domain with fixed observed mileage. It does not
cover mileage concealment, unseen values or the original full four-field
game, and this pilot supplies no confirmatory or universal method advantage.


\clearpage
\section{Controlled completion protocols and full comparison evidence}\label{app:completion_methods}
\subsection{Recent-method identity and training boundaries}
The adapters use the same fixed price backends as ACO-1, not each paper's
original predictive task. SEFA, GDFS, DIME and explanation-ranked AFA learn
acquisition using four training-quantile price classes; their scores feed a
shared hard-capacity, one-query allocator. This does not make them native
strategic regressors. Nonquery context is available, natural absence is
unrecoverable, and deployment scores do not observe a hidden value or its
missingness cause. Hidden-mileage perturbation checks left the corresponding
public-state scores unchanged. Predictive information scores and policy
probabilities are used as cross-listing priority proxies without a theorem of
cross-listing cardinal comparability.

SEFA used its official MIT implementation, commit
\texttt{3af8da2}, configurations 1/5/9,
120 epochs per trial and their original latent-sampling settings. GDFS and DIME
used the comparison implementations in that repository, retaining their
relaxed-one-hot/temperature and entropy-scaled value/epsilon-greedy cores.
Their configurations 1/5/9 use 80 predictor-pretraining epochs and 15 epochs
per annealing stage. The task adapter starts from legal public reports and
trains at most two recoveries, uses Adam and stagewise best validation states,
and does not reproduce the original learning-rate scheduler. This is a sourced
task adaptation, not an unchanged original-author reproduction.

Explanation-ranked AFA used the official repository commit
\texttt{d08f39a}, its tabular MLP and
three-layer, four-head GPT, hidden width 128 and embedding width 64.
A fitting-partition 250-tree CatBoost classifier supplies TreeSHAP rankings;
field-value and missing-indicator absolute contributions for the training label
are summed. Natural missingness and numeric category coding are task
adaptations. The stages comprise 250 masked-predictor, 200 ranking-supervised
and 16 policy-trajectory epochs, with at most two recoveries per training
trajectory and a fixed initial action marker at history-free deployment.
Learning rates $0.001,0.0005,0.0003$ form a fixed local grid; the last is an
additional sensitivity value rather than an original default.

All four classification adapters select among three configurations using the
same training-only 80:20 split and then refit on all training records. SEFA
uses its masked validation NLL and selected epoch, while the other three use
mean pre/post-query NLL and their full fixed schedule. Thus selection effort
is documented but their validation objectives are not identical. No evaluation
outcome is used to select a configuration. ACO-1 instead retains the declared
training-bank rule and fixed neighbor sensitivities described in
Appendix~\ref{app:comparators}. These differences, the fixed 1,000-record
completion cohort, and three fitting seeds bound cross-family conclusions.

\subsection{Full component removals and parameter choice}
$S$ switches suppression rather than ordinary-benefit score training, $F$
switches feedback, and $G$ switches the gap penalty. All $2^3$ combinations
share the role separation, epoch budget and reset schedule. The conventional
backbone, mask augmentation, prediction/attack-error and anchor terms remain
when all three switches are removed. Figure~\ref{fig:completion_ablation}
therefore is a complete component ablation, not a comparison with an untrained
or native predictor.

}
\caption{Complete three-component ablation at 10\% capacity. Circles retain three paired seed differences from S1F1G1 and diamonds show their means. Negative differences indicate lower inflation than the full switched configuration. S/F/G denote suppression-score, feedback and gap switches. Neither number of cells nor number of components is an independent sample size or an innovation count. All nonconverged responses remain in the plot.}
\label{fig:completion_ablation}
\end{figure}

Core sensitivity varied gap weight over $0,0.1,0.25,0.5,1,2$, tolerance over
$0,0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1$, and one/two/four reset-feedback rounds at fixed total
12 residual epochs. The design values 0.5 and 0.02 are fixed engineering
choices on the log-quotation scale, not optimal values estimated from these
curves. Increases in gap weight reduce inflation means in this grid, but
inflation alone cannot select a default. Figure~\ref{fig:completion_parameters}
retains both evaluated capacities. A further 144 cells vary anchor weight,
attack-loss weight, learning rate, epochs, action-head tree count and hidden
width one at a time (Figure~\ref{fig:completion_stability}). The defaults are
inserted from the exactly matched S1F1G1 branch, not retrained or interpolated.
All error, failure and convergence metrics remain in the source tables.

\caption{Core parameter sensitivity under the original weak-feedback training, at 10\% (top) and 30\% (bottom) capacity. Means and full ranges over three fixed fitting seeds. The horizontal axis shows actual evaluated settings, joined by straight lines. Changing round count preserves 12 total residual epochs but changes the optimizer/reset schedule. The plots describe sensitivity and do not select an evaluation-best default.}
\label{fig:completion_parameters}
\end{figure}

\caption{Additional implementation sensitivity at 10\% capacity. Each point is normalized within source/seed to the unchanged matched default, then averaged; shading is the full seed range. All six parameter axes show actual settings, including the exact default. The learning-rate axis is logarithmic and strictly positive. These inflation curves are accompanied by error/failure metrics in source data; a low ratio alone does not establish a better model.}
\label{fig:completion_stability}
\end{figure}

\subsection{Availability-shift stress and source-data coverage}
The availability stress study held fitted models fixed and additionally removed
0, 10\% or 30\% of originally observed query fields using nested stable hashes
of listing and field. Targets were unchanged; newly missing values could not
be recovered. Seven mechanisms (four search/refresh variants, matched
no-feedback, risk and benefit) yielded 189 cells: 63 reused and 126 new
evaluations, with no refitting. Reports were reoptimized after the shift.
This is a controlled availability perturbation, not a new market, MNAR model
or actual-seller validation. Paired metrics, recovery paths and all three
seed values remain in the evidence files. RPL additionally retained low/middle/high
price groups using training-only 10th/90th quantiles and naturally
complete/missing groups; these shared-population subgroups are descriptive.

\subsection{Primary recent-family numerical comparison}
Table~\ref{tab:recent_primary} reports all five family means rather than only
winners. Currency MAE is not pooled across sources. Identical honest MAE within
a frozen backend is expected and does not demonstrate equal attacked error.
